{"etymology_number":"1","forms":[{"form":"winds","tags":["present","singular","third-person"]},{"form":"winding","tags":["participle","present"]},{"form":"winded","tags":["participle","past"]},{"form":"winded","tags":["past"]},{"form":"wound","tags":["participle","past","proscribed"]},{"form":"wound","tags":["past","proscribed"]},{"form":"winde","tags":["alternative","obsolete"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"verb","senses":[{"glosses":["To blow air through a wind instrument or horn to make a sound."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The boxer was winded during round two.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To cause (someone) to become breathless, as by a blow to the abdomen, or by physical exertion, running, etc."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"glosses":["To cause a baby to bring up wind by patting its back after being fed."],"tags":["UK","transitive"]},{"glosses":["To turn a boat or ship around, so that the wind strikes it on the opposite side."],"tags":["UK","transitive"]},{"glosses":["To expose to the wind; to winnow; to ventilate."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The hounds winded the game.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To perceive or follow by scent."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"glosses":["To rest (a horse, etc.) in order to allow the breath to be recovered; to breathe."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"glosses":["To turn a windmill so that its sails face into the wind."],"tags":["transitive"]}],"sounds":[{"enpr":"wĭnd","tags":["General-American","Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/ˈwɪnd/","tags":["General-American","Received-Pronunciation"]},{"enpr":"wīnd","tags":["archaic"]},{"ipa":"/ˈwaɪ̯nd/","tags":["archaic"]}],"upstream_line":35412,"upstream_offset":244961502,"upstream_record_sha256":"13d1755423bc05b560d7023ea788100452b0c7bb046d957c6adcf5e8e71376af","word":"wind"}
